### cld-overview
Investigation: ACME CLD, Coflow Laminar Diffusion Flame (PSI-21)
Topic: overview and data limits
Sources: Challenge brief 'Flame in Freefall: SI-Powered Fire Safety Insights'; PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files); PSI-21 data and mfcSetpoints CSV files

PSI-21 is the ACME Coflow Laminar Diffusion Flame (CLD) investigation: laminar gas-jet diffusion flames burned in a coflow
in the Combustion Integrated Rack on the International Space Station with the modular ACME hardware. [Source: Challenge brief 'Flame in Freefall: SI-Powered Fire Safety Insights']
Each test point has a data file (a time series of about 125 readings per second on 36 channels) and a set point file (the commanded
flow of each mass-flow controller, in volts). [Source: PSI-21 data and mfcSetpoints CSV files]
This corpus summarises 385 test points from 33 test days, recorded between 2017-11-15 and 2021-03-04. Only test points
that have both a data file and a set point file were used. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]
A flame was detected from the centre radiometer in 360 of the 385 test points and not in 25. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files); detection rule from cld_prepare.py; threshold is an assumption]
The thermocouples responded (range above 0.01 V) in only 1 test point(s), so the thermocouple channels are mostly not useful for these tests. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]
The electric field was off in 385 of 385 test points (header EField = 0), so no electric-field effect is expected. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files); header of the data files]

IMPORTANT LIMITS: flows and set points are in volts, not physical units; the *_approx_slpm values assume 0 to 5 V spans zero to full scale
with no gas correction. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files); assumption] The data files carry no fuel concentration, so concentration is only known per test day (see the test-day chunks).
Several test point names contain "MFCA1" (for example 18008MFCA1_1); these look like flow-controller checks and often show no flame, but that
is an inference from the names. [Source: assumption]

-----
### cld-glossary
Investigation: ACME CLD, Coflow Laminar Diffusion Flame (PSI-21)
Topic: columns, codes and detection rules
Sources: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv); PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)

Test point: one recording, named by the test day code plus a suffix (for example 17342C5). [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]
Test day code: year (two digits) followed by the three-digit day of the year, for example 17342 is day 342 of 2017. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv), Notes column]
MFC (mass flow controller): the A1, A2 and A3 controllers; A3 is the fuel controller in the data files, and each has a full scale (maximum flow in
slpm, nitrogen basis) and a gas code. The gas code meanings are not decoded here. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv), Notes column; data file headers]
Set point (V): commanded controller voltage; measured flow plateau (V) is the typical measured voltage while the flow is on. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]
approx slpm: measured flow voltage divided by 5 and multiplied by the full scale. [Source: computed by cld_prepare.py; assumption]
Radiometer rise (V): the centre radiometer's peak rise above its baseline; a flame is called "detected" when the rise exceeds 0.02 V. [Source: cld_prepare.py; threshold is an assumption]
Flame on/off time: first and last time the radiometer rise stays above 25% of its peak rise. [Source: cld_prepare.py; threshold is an assumption]
Thermocouple responded: the largest thermocouple range during the test exceeded 0.01 V. [Source: cld_prepare.py; threshold is an assumption]
Fuel concentration: fuel diluted with nitrogen on a volume basis; it varied between tests and sometimes during tests. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv), Notes column]
Flames ignited (experiment table): the number ignited on that test day; it is counted differently from the radiometer-based detections here. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv), Notes column; PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]

### cld-type-ethylene-based-test
Investigation: ACME CLD, Coflow Laminar Diffusion Flame (PSI-21)
Topic: summary of Ethylene based test
Sources: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv); PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)

Ethylene based test: 14 test days (18145, 18151, 18177, 18186, 18192, 18199, 18214, 18218, 18235, 18239, 18241, 18249, 18253, 18257). [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv)]
The experiment table lists 162 flames ignited on these days. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv)]
Fuel bottles: 100% C2H10, 100% C2H11, 100% C2H4, 100% C2H5, 100% C2H6, 100% C2H7, 100% C2H8, 100% C2H9, 50% C2H4, 50% C2H5, 50% C2H6, 50% C2H7, 50% C2H8, 50% C2H9. Fuel concentration ranges: 10%-20%, 10%-30%, 100%, 15%-20%, 15%-30%, 40%, 40%-100%, 50%, 50%-60%, 60%-100%, 80%, 80%-100%. [Source: PSI-21 Experimental table (PSI-21_Experimental_table_CLD_Flame.csv)]
163 test points from these days are summarised here; a flame was detected in 163 of them. [Source: PSI-21 cld_testpoints.csv (made by cld_prepare.py from each test point's data and mfcSetpoints files)]
